# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title hexgame Model

abstract class AgentBase {
    colour : Colour
    hashCode() : int
    makeMove(turn : int, board : Board, oppMove : Move) : Move
    oppColour() : Colour
}
class Board {
    size : int
    tiles : tilearray
    winner : Colour
    clearTiles() : void
    clone() : Board
    dfsColour(x : int, y : int, colour : Colour) : void
    equals(o : EJavaObject) : boolean
    hasEnded(colour : Colour) : boolean
    printBoard() : String
    setTileColour(x : int, y : int, colour : Colour) : void
}
class Game {
    currentPlayer : Colour
    hasSwapped : boolean
    logIsStderr : boolean
    logWriter : PrintWriter
    maximumTime : long
    players : EMap
    startTime : long
    turn : int
    board : Board
    helper : HexHelper
    player1 : Player
    player2 : Player
    endGame(status : EndState) : EMap
    formatResult(player1Name : String, player2Name : String, winner : String, winMethod : String, player1MoveTime : double, player2MoveTime : double, player1Turn : int, player2Turn : int, totalTurns : double, totalTime : double) : EMap
    init(player1 : Player, player2 : Player, boardSize : int, logWriter : PrintWriter, logIsStderr : boolean) : void
    isValidMove(move : Move, turn : int, board : Board) : boolean
    makeMove(m : Move) : void
    nsToS(t : long) : double
    play() : EMap
    run() : EMap
}
class HexHelper {
    boardFromString(stringInput : String, boardSize : int) : Board
    colourFromChar(c : char) : Colour
    colourGetChar(colour : Colour) : String
    colourOpposite(colour : Colour) : Colour
    createBoard(boardSize : int) : Board
}
class Move {
    x : int
    y : int
    toString() : String
}
class Player {
    moveTime : long
    name : String
    turn : int
    agent : AgentBase
}
class Tile {
    NEIGHBOUR_COUNT : int
    colour : Colour
    iDisplacements : IntArrary
    jDisplacements : IntArrary
    visited : boolean
    x : int
    y : int
    clearVisit() : void
    visit() : void
}
enum Colour {
    EMPTY
    RED
    BLUE
}
enum EndState {
    WIN
    TIMEOUT
    BAD_MOVE
    FAILED_LOAD
}

Game *-- Board : board
Game --> HexHelper : helper
Game --> Player : player1
Game --> Player : player2
Player --> AgentBase : agent
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

# Operation Specifications
Board::clearTiles
Summary: Clears the visited flag on every Tile of this board so that the board is ready for a new traversal (e.g., a fresh win-detection search), without altering tile colours or coordinates.
Algorithm:
  1. Iterate over all entries (or indices) of tiles in this board.
  2. For each Tile, invoke clearVisit() to set visited = false.
  3. Continue until every tile has been processed.
Related Features: tiles, winner
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: The board must be fully constructed with all tiles initialized; tiles array must contain valid Tile instances.
Post-condition: Every Tile in tiles has its visited flag set to false; tile colours and coordinates remain unchanged; winner state is not affected by this operation.

Board::equals
Summary: Determines whether this Board is equal to another object by comparing size, every Tile (colour and coordinates), and the winner; used to support testing and snapshot comparison.
Algorithm:
  1. If the other object is the same reference (this == o), return true.
  2. If the other object is null or not a Board, return false.
  3. Compare size fields; if different, return false.
  4. Compare the winner fields; if different, return false.
  5. For each tile at (x, y), compare colour (and optionally visited state); if any differ, return false.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: size, tiles, winner
Related Classes: Tile, Colour, EJavaObject
Input:
  - o: EJavaObject, the object to compare with this Board, expected to be a Board instance for equality.
Output: boolean; true when the other object is a Board with the same size, the same tile colours at every position, and the same winner; otherwise false.
Pre-condition: Both boards (this and o) must be fully initialized with tiles.
Post-condition: Neither board is modified; result reflects structural equality between the two board states.

Board::setTileColour
Summary: Places a given colour stone at the specified board coordinate, mutating that Tile's colour while leaving other Tile attributes (coordinates, neighbour displacements, visited flag) intact.
Algorithm:
  1. Locate the Tile at position (x, y) in tiles.
  2. If (x, y) is within bounds and the tile exists, set the tile's colour to the given colour.
  3. Otherwise, perform no change (or raise an error, depending on caller expectations).
Related Features: tiles, size
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - x: int, the x-coordinate of the target tile, range [0, size-1].
  - y: int, the y-coordinate of the target tile, range [0, size-1].
  - colour: Colour, the colour to be placed at that tile (Colour.RED, Colour.BLUE, or Colour.EMPTY to clear).
Output: void
Pre-condition: tiles must be initialized; (x, y) must be within board bounds before invoking this operation.
Post-condition: The tile at (x, y) now has its colour field set to the supplied colour; other tiles are unchanged; visited flags of the affected tile and others are not automatically reset.

Board::hasEnded
Summary: Determines whether the given colour has already won the game on this board; sets the winner field when a winning connection is found, and returns true/false accordingly.
Algorithm:
  1. For Colour.RED:
     a. Iterate over all tiles on the top edge (y = 0) that are coloured RED.
     b. Clear all tile visited flags via clearTiles().
     c. Run dfsColour(x, 0, RED) for each starting tile.
     d. Check whether any tile on the bottom edge (y = size - 1) is marked visited.
     e. If yes, set winner = RED and return true; otherwise return false.
  2. For Colour.BLUE:
     a. Iterate over all tiles on the left edge (x = 0) that are coloured BLUE.
     b. Clear all tile visited flags via clearTiles().
     c. Run dfsColour(0, y, BLUE) for each starting tile.
     d. Check whether any tile on the right edge (x = size - 1) is marked visited.
     e. If yes, set winner = BLUE and return true; otherwise return false.
  3. For Colour.EMPTY or other invalid input, return false.
Related Features: tiles, size, winner
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - colour: Colour, the colour whose win condition is being tested; must be Colour.RED or Colour.BLUE.
Output: boolean; true if the colour has formed a connected path across the board (top-bottom for RED, left-right for BLUE), false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The board must be fully initialized and consistent; tile colours and neighbour displacements must be valid.
Post-condition: If a winning path is found, winner is set to the given colour; clearTiles() is called as part of the algorithm so the board's visited flags are reset.

Board::dfsColour
Summary: Performs a depth-first search from position (x, y) over Hex-style adjacency (six neighbours) and records visited tiles; used as a step inside win detection to mark all tiles reachable by the given colour.
Algorithm:
  1. Retrieve the Tile at position (x, y); if it is null or out of bounds, return.
  2. If the tile's colour does not equal the given colour, or the tile is already visited, return.
  3. Mark the tile as visited (tile.visit()).
  4. For each of the six neighbour displacement pairs (iDisplacements[k], jDisplacements[k]):
     a. Compute neighbour coordinates (x + di, y + dj).
     b. Recursively call dfsColour(neighbourX, neighbourY, colour).
  5. Return.
Related Features: tiles, size
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - x: int, x-coordinate on the board, range [0, size-1].
  - y: int, y-coordinate on the board, range [0, size-1].
  - colour: Colour, the colour whose connected component should be traversed.
Output: void
Pre-condition: tiles must be initialized; clearTiles() should typically have been called beforehand; (x, y) must be within board boundaries when first called.
Post-condition: All tiles in the connected Hex-neighbour same-colour component containing (x, y) have their visited flag set to true; other tiles are not affected.

Board::clone
Summary: Creates and returns a deep copy of this Board instance so that independent tests, simulations, or agent reasoning can operate on a duplicate without affecting the original state.
Algorithm:
  1. Allocate a new Board instance of the same size.
  2. For each position (x, y) in this board, copy the corresponding Tile's attributes (x, y, colour, visited, neighbour displacement arrays) into the new board.
  3. Set the winner field of the clone to match the winner of this board.
  4. Return the new Board object.
Related Features: size, tiles, winner
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: None
Output: A new Board instance that is a deep copy of this board; its tiles, colours, and winner are independent copies of the originals.
Pre-condition: The original board must be in a valid, fully-initialized state with consistent tile data.
Post-condition: The returned Board is independent of this board; modifications on either one (e.g., setting a tile colour or winner) do not affect the other.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

    BAD_MOVE
    FAILED_LOAD
}

RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

# Operation Specifications
Board::clearTiles
Summary: Clears the visited flag on every Tile of this board so that the board is ready for a new traversal (e.g., a fresh win-detection search), without altering tile colours or coordinates.
Algorithm:
  1. Iterate over all entries (or indices) of tiles in this board.
  2. For each Tile, invoke clearVisit() to set visited = false.
  3. Continue until every tile has been processed.
Related Features: tiles
Related Classes: Tile
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: The board must be fully constructed with all tiles initialized; tiles array must contain valid Tile instances.
Post-condition: Every Tile in tiles has its visited flag set to false; tile colours and coordinates remain unchanged; winner state is not affected by this operation.

Board::equals
Summary: Determines whether this Board is equal to another object by comparing size, the colour of every Tile, and the winner; used to support testing and snapshot comparison.
Algorithm:
  1. If the other object is the same reference (this == o), return true.
  2. If the other object is null or not a Board, return false.
  3. Compare size fields; if different, return false.
  4. Compare the winner fields; if different, return false.
  5. For each tile at (x, y), compare colour; if any differ, return false.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: size, tiles, winner
Related Classes: Tile, Colour, EJavaObject
Input:
  - o: EJavaObject, the object to compare with this Board, expected to be a Board instance for equality.
Output: boolean; true when the other object is a Board with the same size, the same tile colours at every position, and the same winner; otherwise false.
Pre-condition: Both boards (this and o) must be fully initialized with tiles.
Post-condition: Neither board is modified; result reflects structural equality between the two board states.

Board::setTileColour
Summary: Places a given colour stone at the specified board coordinate, mutating that Tile's colour while leaving other Tile attributes (coordinates, neighbour displacements, visited flag) intact.
Algorithm:
  1. Locate the Tile at position (x, y) in tiles.
  2. If (x, y) is within bounds and the tile exists, set the tile's colour to the given colour.
  3. Otherwise, perform no change (or raise an error, depending on caller expectations).
Related Features: tiles, size
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - x: int, the x-coordinate of the target tile, range [0, size-1].
  - y: int, the y-coordinate of the target tile, range [0, size-1].
  - colour: Colour, the colour to be placed at that tile (Colour.RED, Colour.BLUE, or Colour.EMPTY to clear).
Output: void
Pre-condition: tiles must be initialized; (x, y) must be within board bounds before invoking this operation.
Post-condition: The tile at (x, y) now has its colour field set to the supplied colour; other tiles are unchanged; visited flags of the affected tile and others are not automatically reset.

Board::hasEnded
Summary: Determines whether the given colour has already won the game on this board; sets the winner field when a winning connection is found, and returns true/false accordingly.
Algorithm:
  1. For Colour.RED:
     a. Iterate over all tiles on the top edge (y = 0) that are coloured RED.
     b. Clear all tile visited flags via clearTiles().
     c. Run dfsColour(x, 0, RED) for each starting tile.
     d. Check whether any tile on the bottom edge (y = size - 1) is marked visited.
     e. If yes, set winner = RED and return true; otherwise return false.
  2. For Colour.BLUE:
     a. Iterate over all tiles on the left edge (x = 0) that are coloured BLUE.
     b. Clear all tile visited flags via clearTiles().
     c. Run dfsColour(0, y, BLUE) for each starting tile.
     d. Check whether any tile on the right edge (x = size - 1) is marked visited.
     e. If yes, set winner = BLUE and return true; otherwise return false.
  3. For Colour.EMPTY or other invalid input, return false.
Related Features: tiles, size, winner
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - colour: Colour, the colour whose win condition is being tested; must be Colour.RED or Colour.BLUE.
Output: boolean; true if the colour has formed a connected path across the board (top-bottom for RED, left-right for BLUE), false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The board must be fully initialized and consistent; tile colours and neighbour displacements must be valid.
Post-condition: If a winning path is found, winner is set to the given colour; clearTiles() is called as part of the algorithm so the board's visited flags are reset.

Board::dfsColour
Summary: Performs a depth-first search from position (x, y) over Hex-style adjacency (six neighbours) and records visited tiles; used as a step inside win detection to mark all tiles reachable by the given colour.
Algorithm:
  1. Retrieve the Tile at position (x, y); if it is null or out of bounds, return.
  2. If the tile's colour does not equal the given colour, or the tile is already visited, return.
  3. Mark the tile as visited (tile.visit()).
  4. For each of the six neighbour displacement pairs (iDisplacements[k], jDisplacements[k]):
     a. Compute neighbour coordinates (x + di, y + dj).
     b. Recursively call dfsColour(neighbourX, neighbourY, colour).
  5. Return.
Related Features: tiles, size
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - x: int, x-coordinate on the board, range [0, size-1].
  - y: int, y-coordinate on the board, range [0, size-1].
  - colour: Colour, the colour whose connected component should be traversed.
Output: void
Pre-condition: tiles must be initialized; clearTiles() should typically have been called beforehand; (x, y) must be within board boundaries when first called.
Post-condition: All tiles in the connected Hex-neighbour same-colour component containing (x, y) have their visited flag set to true; other tiles are not affected.

Board::clone
Summary: Creates and returns a deep copy of this Board instance so that independent tests, simulations, or agent reasoning can operate on a duplicate without affecting the original state.
Algorithm:
  1. Allocate a new Board instance of the same size.
  2. For each position (x, y) in this board, copy the corresponding Tile's attributes (x, y, colour, visited, neighbour displacement arrays) into the new board.
  3. Set the winner field of the clone to match the winner of this board.
  4. Return the new Board object.
Related Features: size, tiles, winner
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: None
Output: A new Board instance that is a deep copy of this board; its tiles, colours, and winner are independent copies of the originals.
Pre-condition: The original board must be in a valid, fully-initialized state with consistent tile data.
Post-condition: The returned Board is independent of this board; modifications on either one (e.g., setting a tile colour or winner) do not affect the other.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

  4. For each of the six standard neighbour displacement pairs in iDisplacements and jDisplacements, compute the neighbour coordinates and recursively invoke dfsColour for that neighbour.
  5. Return after all six neighbours have been processed.
Related Features: tiles, size
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - x: int, zero-based x-coordinate from which traversal begins; the initial value must be within [0, size - 1] unless the implementation permits the method to be called as part of external traversal.
  - y: int, zero-based y-coordinate from which traversal begins; the initial value must be within [0, size - 1] unless the implementation permits the method to be called as part of external traversal.
  - colour: Colour, the colour whose connected component is traversed; normally Colour.RED or Colour.BLUE.
Output: void
Pre-condition: The board is fully initialized, and colour identifies a valid occupied colour. The board traversal state should have been cleared before this search.

Algorithm:
  1. Allocate a new Board with the same size and no initial winner.
  2. For each position (x, y), copy the Tile's x and y coordinates and colour into the corresponding tile of the new board.
  3. Copy this board's visited state to the corresponding tile in the new board.
  4. Do not share Tile objects or neighbour-displacement arrays with the original.
  5. Set the new board's winner to this board's winner.
  6. Return the new Board.
Related Features: size, tiles, winner
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: None
Output: A new Board instance with the same size, tile coordinates, tile colours, visited state, and winner as this Board, but independent tiles and traversal state.
Pre-condition: This Board is fully initialized with a consistent size and complete tiles array.
Post-condition: The returned board is an independent state copy. Subsequent changes to either board's tile colours, visited flags, or winner do not affect the other board., org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@2282400e (name: equals) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 1, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Determines whether this Board and another object represent the same board state. Equality considers the board size, the colour and visited state of every corresponding Tile, and the winner; the neighbour displacement arrays are not required to be compared separately because identical tile coordinates imply the same standard six Hex directions.
Algorithm:
  1. If the other object is the same reference as this board, return true.
  2. If the other object is null or is not a Board, return false.
  3. Compare the size fields; if they differ, return false.
  4. Compare the winner fields; if they differ, return false.
  5. For every coordinate (x, y), retrieve the corresponding tiles and compare their colour and visited values. If any corresponding value differs, return false.
  6. Return true.

  2. Locate the Tile at (x, y) in tiles.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

# Operation Specifications
AgentBase::hashCode
Summary: Computes and returns the hash code of this AgentBase instance based on its colour attribute, in order to support correct behaviour of AgentBase objects when used as keys in hash-based collections.
Algorithm:
  1. Retrieve the colour attribute of this AgentBase.
  2. Combine the colour value (typically using Objects.hash or equivalent) with an appropriate prime multiplier to produce an int hash.
  3. Return the resulting int value.
Related Features: colour
Related Classes: Colour
Input: None
Output: An int hash value representing this AgentBase instance; range is the full 32-bit signed integer range.
Pre-condition: The AgentBase instance must be in a valid state with colour properly initialized to a non-null Colour value.
Post-condition: The AgentBase instance remains unchanged; the returned hash value follows the contract that equal AgentBase objects (same colour) produce equal hash codes.

AgentBase::oppColour
Summary: Returns the opposite colour of this agent's colour (RED returns BLUE, BLUE returns RED); raises an error if the agent's colour is invalid or EMPTY.
Algorithm:
  1. Examine this agent's colour field.
  2. If colour is Colour.RED, return Colour.BLUE.
  3. If colour is Colour.BLUE, return Colour.RED.
  4. Otherwise (colour is Colour.EMPTY or null), raise an error/exception indicating that an opposite colour cannot be determined.
Related Features: colour
Related Classes: Colour
Input: None
Output: A Colour value representing the opposite side (Colour.BLUE when the agent is RED, or Colour.RED when the agent is BLUE); must never be Colour.EMPTY.
Pre-condition: This agent's colour must be a valid player colour (Colour.RED or Colour.BLUE); it must not be Colour.EMPTY or any other invalid value.
Post-condition: The returned Colour is guaranteed to be the opposite valid player colour; the agent instance remains unchanged.

Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

# Operation Specifications
Board::clearTiles
Summary: Clears the visited flag on every tile in the board. This is used between win-detection traversals so that DFS or BFS marks left by a previous search do not leak into later ones.
Algorithm:
  1. Iterate over every Tile in tiles (rows 0..size-1, columns 0..size-1).
  2. For each tile, invoke tile.clearVisit() so that visited is reset to false.
Related Features: Board.tiles, Tile.visited, Tile.clearVisit
Related Classes: Tile
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: Board must be initialised; tiles array must not be null.
Post-condition: Every Tile in the board has visited == false; no other tile state (colour, coordinate) is altered.

Board::equals
Summary: Compares this board with another object for state equality. Used to detect repeated board states and to support testing of agents that look at past positions.
Algorithm:
  1. If o is the same reference (this), return true.
  2. If o is null or not a Board, return false.
  3. If sizes differ, return false.
  4. For every (x, y), compare the colour of the corresponding Tile. If any colour differs, return false.
  5. Otherwise return true.
Related Features: Board.size, Board.tiles, Board.winner, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: o : EJavaObject — any object; meaningful only when it is a Board.
Output: boolean — true iff o is a Board of the same size with identical colour on every cell.
Pre-condition: Both boards (when applicable) must be fully initialised.
Post-condition: No state is mutated.

Board::setTileColour
Summary: Sets the colour of the tile at coordinate (x, y). Used to apply a legal move to the board.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that (x, y) is within bounds 0..size-1.
  2. Retrieve the Tile at (x, y).
  3. Assign the given colour to tile.colour.
Related Features: Board.tiles, Board.size, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - x : int — column coordinate, 0 ≤ x < size.
  - y : int — row coordinate, 0 ≤ y < size.
  - colour : Colour — the colour to assign to the tile (typically RED or BLUE).
Output: void
Pre-condition: (x, y) must be in range; the target tile should currently be EMPTY when applying a real move (the Game layer is responsible for that rule).
Post-condition: The tile at (x, y) now has the supplied colour; all other tiles are unchanged.

Board::hasEnded
Summary: Determines whether the given colour has already won the game by forming a connected path across the board in its winning direction. Also returns true if a previous winner is stored.
Algorithm:
  1. If board.winner is already set to colour, return true.
  2. clearTiles() to ensure a clean traversal state.
  3. If colour == RED:
       For each x in 0..size-1, if the top-row tile (x, 0) is RED, call dfsColour(x, 0, RED).
       Then check whether any bottom-row tile (x, size-1) has visited == true. If yes, set winner = RED and return true.
  4. If colour == BLUE:
       For each y in 0..size-1, if the left-column tile (0, y) is BLUE, call dfsColour(0, y, BLUE).
       Then check whether any right-column tile (size-1, y) has visited == true. If yes, set winner = BLUE and return true.
  5. Otherwise return false.
Related Features: Board.tiles, Board.winner, Board.size, Board.dfsColour, Board.clearTiles, Tile.visited, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: colour : Colour — the player colour to test for a win.
Output: boolean — true if that colour has a connected path from its start edge to its end edge.
Pre-condition: Board must be initialised and have tiles with correct colours and neighbour offsets.
Post-condition: Visited flags are set on tiles reachable from the start edge; winner may be set if a win is detected.

Board::dfsColour
Summary: Performs a depth-first search over the board, starting from coordinate (x, y), visiting only tiles whose colour matches the supplied colour. This is the core traversal used by win detection.
Algorithm:
  1. If (x, y) is out of bounds, return.
  2. Retrieve the Tile at (x, y). If the tile is null, already visited, or has a different colour, return.
  3. Mark the tile as visited (visit()).
  4. For each of the six neighbour offsets (iDisp[k], jDisp[k]) of the current tile, recursively call dfsColour(x + iDisp[k], y + jDisp[k], colour).
Related Features: Board.tiles, Tile.iDisplacements, Tile.jDisplacements, Tile.visited, Tile.visit, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile
Input:
  - x : int — column coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
  - y : int — row coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
  - colour : Colour — the colour whose connectivity is being explored (RED or BLUE).
Output: void
Pre-condition: Board tiles must be initialised; (x, y) should be in-range and contain the given colour for a meaningful call. Traversal marks should typically be cleared beforehand via clearTiles().
Post-condition: All tiles reachable from (x, y) via same-colour six-way adjacency are marked visited; the board's logical state is unchanged.

Board::clone
Summary: Produces a deep copy of the board so that agents receive a snapshot they can analyse without mutating the real game state.
Algorithm:
  1. Create a new Board instance of the same size.
  2. For each tile coordinate (x, y) in the original, copy colour, coordinate and visited state into the new board's tile at the same position.
  3. Copy the winner field if it is non-null.
  4. Return the new Board.
Related Features: Board.size, Board.tiles, Board.winner, Tile.colour, Tile.x, Tile.y, Tile.visited
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: None
Output: Board — a new, independent Board instance with identical observable state.
Pre-condition: Original board must be in a consistent state (all tiles initialised).
Post-condition: The returned board has equal state to the original but is a separate object; mutating it does not affect the original.

Post-condition: Visited flags are set on tiles reachable from the start edge; winner may be set if a win is detected.

Board::dfsColour
Summary: Performs a depth-first search over the board, starting from coordinate (x, y), visiting only tiles whose colour matches the supplied colour. This is the core traversal used by win detection.
Algorithm:
  1. If (x, y) is out of bounds, return.
  2. Retrieve the Tile at (x, y). If the tile is null, already visited, or has a different colour, return.
  3. Mark the tile as visited (visit()).
  4. For each of the six neighbour offsets (iDisp[k], jDisp[k]) of the current tile, recursively call dfsColour(x + iDisp[k], y + jDisp[k], colour).
Related Features: Board.tiles, Tile.iDisplacements, Tile.jDisplacements, Tile.visited, Tile.visit, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile
Input:
  - x : int — column coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
  - y : int — row coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
  - colour : Colour — the colour whose connectivity is being explored (RED or BLUE).
Output: void
Pre-condition: Board tiles must be initialised; (x, y) should be in-range and contain the given colour for a meaningful call. Traversal marks should typically be cleared beforehand via clearTiles().
Post-condition: All tiles reachable from (x, y) via same-colour six-way adjacency are marked visited; the board's logical state is unchanged.

Board::clone
Summary: Produces a deep copy of the board so that agents receive a snapshot they can analyse without mutating the real game state.
Algorithm:
  1. Create a new Board instance of the same size.
  2. For each tile coordinate (x, y) in the original, copy colour, coordinate and visited state into the new board's tile at the same position.
  3. Copy the winner field if it is non-null.
  4. Return the new Board.
Related Features: Board.size, Board.tiles, Board.winner, Tile.colour, Tile.x, Tile.y, Tile.visited
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: None
Output: Board — a new, independent Board instance with identical observable state.
Pre-condition: Original board must be in a consistent state (all tiles initialised).
Post-condition: The returned board has equal state to the original but is a separate object; mutating it does not affect the original.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

# Operation Specifications
Board::clearTiles
Summary: Clears the visited flag on every tile in the board. This is used between win-detection traversals so that DFS or BFS marks left by a previous search do not leak into later ones.
Algorithm:
  1. Iterate over every Tile in tiles (rows 0..size-1, columns 0..size-1).
  2. For each tile, invoke tile.clearVisit() so that visited is reset to false.
Related Features: Board.tiles, Tile.visited, Tile.clearVisit
Related Classes: Tile
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: Board must be initialised; tiles array must not be null.
Post-condition: Every Tile in the board has visited == false; no other tile state (colour, coordinate) is altered.

Board::equals
Summary: Compares this board with another object for state equality. Used to detect repeated board states and to support testing of agents that look at past positions.
Algorithm:
  1. If o is the same reference (this), return true.
  2. If o is null or not a Board, return false.
  3. If sizes differ, return false.
  4. For every (x, y), compare the colour of the corresponding Tile. If any colour differs, return false.
  5. Otherwise return true.
Related Features: Board.size, Board.tiles, Board.winner, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: o : EJavaObject — any object; meaningful only when it is a Board.
Output: boolean — true iff o is a Board of the same size with identical colour on every cell.
Pre-condition: Both boards (when applicable) must be fully initialised.
Post-condition: No state is mutated.

Board::setTileColour
Summary: Sets the colour of the tile at coordinate (x, y). Used to apply a legal move to the board.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that (x, y) is within bounds 0..size-1.
  2. Retrieve the Tile at (x, y).
  3. Assign the given colour to tile.colour.
Related Features: Board.tiles, Board.size, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - x : int — column coordinate, 0 ≤ x < size.
  - y : int — row coordinate, 0 ≤ y < size.
  - colour : Colour — the colour to assign to the tile (typically RED or BLUE).
Output: void
Pre-condition: (x, y) must be in range; the target tile should currently be EMPTY when applying a real move (the Game layer is responsible for that rule).
Post-condition: The tile at (x, y) now has the supplied colour; all other tiles are unchanged.

Board::hasEnded
Summary: Determines whether the given colour has won by forming a connected path between its designated board edges. It also returns true when the board has previously recorded the same winning colour.
Algorithm:
  1. If board.winner is non-EMPTY and is not the supplied colour, return false.
  2. If board.winner equals the supplied colour, return true.
  3. Invoke clearTiles() to remove traversal marks from any earlier search.
  4. If colour is RED, perform a six-neighbour DFS from every RED tile in the top row and determine whether the bottom row is reachable. If colour is BLUE, perform the corresponding search from every BLUE tile in the left column and determine whether the right column is reachable.
  5. If the target edge is reached, set winner to colour and return true; otherwise return false.
Related Features: Board.winner, Board.tiles, Board.size, Board.dfsColour, Board.clearTiles, Tile.visited, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - colour : Colour — the player colour to test; normally RED or BLUE.
Output: boolean — true if that colour has connected its starting edge to its target edge, including when that colour is already stored as the winner; false if it has not or if a different colour is already the winner.
Pre-condition: The board must be fully initialised, and colour must be RED or BLUE.
Post-condition: The traversal marks generated by this check remain on the board. If a win is found, Board.winner is set to colour; otherwise, the existing winner state is preserved.

Board::dfsColour
Summary: Performs a depth-first search over the board, starting from coordinate (x, y), visiting only tiles whose colour matches the supplied colour. This is the core traversal used by win detection.
Algorithm:
  1. If (x, y) is out of bounds, return.
  2. Retrieve the Tile at (x, y). If the tile is null, already visited, or has a different colour, return.
  3. Mark the tile as visited (visit()).
  4. For each of the six neighbour offsets (iDisp[k], jDisp[k]) of the current tile, recursively call dfsColour(x + iDisp[k], y + jDisp[k], colour).
Related Features: Board.tiles, Tile.iDisplacements, Tile.jDisplacements, Tile.visited, Tile.visit, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile
Input:
  - x : int — column coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
  - y : int — row coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
  - colour : Colour — the colour whose connectivity is being explored (RED or BLUE).
Output: void
Pre-condition: Board tiles must be initialised; (x, y) should be in-range and contain the given colour for a meaningful call. Traversal marks should typically be cleared beforehand via clearTiles().
Post-condition: All tiles reachable from (x, y) via same-colour six-way adjacency are marked visited; the board's logical state is unchanged.

Board::clone
Summary: Produces a deep copy of the board so that agents receive a snapshot they can analyse without mutating the real game state.
Algorithm:
  1. Create a new Board instance of the same size.
  2. For each tile coordinate (x, y) in the original, copy colour, coordinate and visited state into the new board's tile at the same position.
  3. Copy the winner field if it is non-null.
  4. Return the new Board.
Related Features: Board.size, Board.tiles, Board.winner, Tile.colour, Tile.x, Tile.y, Tile.visited
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: None
Output: Board — a new, independent Board instance with identical observable state.
Pre-condition: Original board must be in a consistent state (all tiles initialised).
Post-condition: The returned board has equal state to the original but is a separate object; mutating it does not affect the original.
