  3. Assign the given colour to tile.colour.
Related Features: Board.tiles, Board.size, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - x : int — column coordinate, 0 ≤ x < size.
  - y : int — row coordinate, 0 ≤ y < size.
  - colour : Colour — the colour to assign to the tile (typically RED or BLUE).
Output: void
Pre-condition: (x, y) must be in range; the target tile should currently be EMPTY when applying a real move (the Game layer is responsible for that rule).
Post-condition: The tile at (x, y) now has the supplied colour; all other tiles are unchanged.

Board::hasEnded
Summary: Determines whether the given colour has won by forming a connected path between its designated board edges. It also returns true when the board has previously recorded the same winning colour.
Algorithm:
  1. If board.winner is non-EMPTY and is not the supplied colour, return false.
  2. If board.winner equals the supplied colour, return true.
  3. Invoke clearTiles() to remove traversal marks from any earlier search.
  4. If colour is RED, perform a six-neighbour DFS from every RED tile in the top row and determine whether the bottom row is reachable. If colour is BLUE, perform the corresponding search from every BLUE tile in the left column and determine whether the right column is reachable.
  5. If the target edge is reached, set winner to colour and return true; otherwise return false.
Related Features: Board.winner, Board.tiles, Board.size, Board.dfsColour, Board.clearTiles, Tile.visited, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input:
  - colour : Colour — the player colour to test; normally RED or BLUE.
Output: boolean — true if that colour has connected its starting edge to its target edge, including when that colour is already stored as the winner; false if it has not or if a different colour is already the winner.
Pre-condition: The board must be fully initialised, and colour must be RED or BLUE.
Post-condition: The traversal marks generated by this check remain on the board. If a win is found, Board.winner is set to colour; otherwise, the existing winner state is preserved.

Board::dfsColour
Summary: Performs a depth-first search over the board, starting from coordinate (x, y), visiting only tiles whose colour matches the supplied colour. This is the core traversal used by win detection.
Algorithm:
  1. If (x, y) is out of bounds, return.
  2. Retrieve the Tile at (x, y). If the tile is null, already visited, or has a different colour, return.
  3. Mark the tile as visited (visit()).
  4. For each of the six neighbour offsets (iDisp[k], jDisp[k]) of the current tile, recursively call dfsColour(x + iDisp[k], y + jDisp[k], colour).
Related Features: Board.tiles, Tile.iDisplacements, Tile.jDisplacements, Tile.visited, Tile.visit, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Tile
Input:
  - x : int — column coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
  - y : int — row coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
  - colour : Colour — the colour whose connectivity is being explored (RED or BLUE).
Output: void
Pre-condition: Board tiles must be initialised; (x, y) should be in-range and contain the given colour for a meaningful call. Traversal marks should typically be cleared beforehand via clearTiles().
Post-condition: All tiles reachable from (x, y) via same-colour six-way adjacency are marked visited; the board's logical state is unchanged.

Board::clone
Summary: Produces a deep copy of the board so that agents receive a snapshot they can analyse without mutating the real game state.
Algorithm:
  1. Create a new Board instance of the same size.
  2. For each tile coordinate (x, y) in the original, copy colour, coordinate and visited state into the new board's tile at the same position.
  3. Copy the winner field if it is non-null.
  4. Return the new Board.
Related Features: Board.size, Board.tiles, Board.winner, Tile.colour, Tile.x, Tile.y, Tile.visited
Related Classes: Tile, Colour
Input: None
Output: Board — a new, independent Board instance with identical observable state.
Pre-condition: Original board must be in a consistent state (all tiles initialised).
Post-condition: The returned board has equal state to the original but is a separate object; mutating it does not affect the original.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

# Operation Specifications
AgentBase::hashCode
Summary: Computes a hash code for the agent derived from its colour, so that agents can be used in hash-based collections (e.g., EMap) and for equality consistency with Board equality logic.
Algorithm:
  1. Return the ordinal value of the agent's colour (Colour.RED, Colour.BLUE, or Colour.EMPTY).
  2. If colour is null, return 0 to keep the contract well-defined.
Related Features: AgentBase.colour
Related Classes: Colour
Input: None
Output: int — a stable hash code; ranges from 0 upward depending on the Colour enum ordinal.
Pre-condition: The agent instance must have a valid colour assigned (not strictly required because null is handled defensively).
Post-condition: The returned value is deterministic for equal agents and consistent across calls within a single execution.

AgentBase::oppColour
Summary: Returns the opposite player colour of the agent (RED ↔ BLUE). This is used to identify the opponent when invoking the agent with the previous move of the other side.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that the agent's colour is not null and is either RED or BLUE.
  2. If colour == RED, return BLUE.
  3. If colour == BLUE, return RED.
  4. Otherwise, signal an error (throw IllegalStateException / IllegalArgumentException) — the requirement states an invalid or missing colour must be treated as an error.
Related Features: AgentBase.colour
Related Classes: Colour
Input: None (operates on the agent's own colour field)
Output: Colour — the opposite player colour (RED if the agent is BLUE, BLUE if the agent is RED).
Pre-condition: AgentBase.colour must be a valid player colour (RED or BLUE).
Post-condition: A Colour different from the agent's own colour is returned; the agent's state is unchanged.

4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;

6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

    y : int

}

    RED
    BLUE

    BAD_MOVE
    FAILED_LOAD
}

Tile::visit

Related Features: Tile.visited, Tile.clearVisit

Output: void

Post-condition: The tile's visited flag is true; all other tile state is unchanged.

Summary: Resets the visited flag on the tile so it can be used again by a fresh traversal.
Algorithm:
  1. Set this.visited = false.
Related Features: Tile.visited
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: The tile's visited flag is false; all other tile state (colour, coordinate, displacements) is unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
Game::play
Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
  2. Loop:
       a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
       b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
       c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.
       d. If active player's moveTime > maximumTime, call endGame(TIMEOUT) and break.
       e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.
       f. Call makeMove(move).
       g. Increment turn.
       h. If board.hasEnded(currentPlayer) is true after the opponent's move, call endGame(WIN) and break.
  3. Return the result map from endGame.
Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.startTime, Game.maximumTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.board, Game.turn, Board.clone, Board.hasEnded, AgentBase.makeMove, Player.moveTime, Player.agent
Related Classes: AgentBase, Player, Board, Move, Colour, EndState
Input: None
Output: EMap — the result of endGame containing the required player1, player2, winner, win_method, player1_move_time, player2_move_time, player1_turns, player2_turns, total_turns, total_game_time entries.
Pre-condition: init(...) must have been called; the players must have valid agents with valid colours.
Post-condition: A complete result map is returned and the game is in an ended state.

Game::makeMove
Summary: Applies a legal move to the game state, updating the board, advancing the turn, toggling the current player, and recording that the swap move has been used if applicable.
Algorithm:
  1. If move represents the swap (x == -1 and y == -1):
       a. Locate the opponent's last move (the only RED tile on the board, since only RED has moved on turn 1).
       b. Set the current player's colour on that tile (i.e., the second player takes over RED's first stone).
       c. Set hasSwapped = true.
  2. Otherwise (normal move):
       a. Call board.setTileColour(move.x, move.y, currentPlayer).
  3. Increment the current player's turn counter (player1.turn or player2.turn) and Game.turn.
  4. Flip currentPlayer to its opposite (RED ↔ BLUE).
Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.board, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.turn, Board.setTileColour, Player.turn
Related Classes: Move, Board, Tile, Colour, Player
Input: m : Move — the validated move to apply.
Output: void
Pre-condition: isValidMove(m, ...) must return true; the game must not already have ended.
Post-condition: The board reflects the move, the active colour has been switched, and the appropriate player's turn count is incremented.

Game::isValidMove
Summary: Checks whether a move is legal in the current game context, considering the turn number, board state and the special swap rule.
Algorithm:
  1. If move is null, return false.
  2. If turn == 2 and move represents the swap move (x == -1 and y == -1) and hasSwapped is false, return true (the second player may swap with the first player's first move).
  3. Otherwise, the move must be a normal coordinate move:
       a. x and y must be within 0..board.size-1.
       b. The tile at (x, y) must currently be EMPTY.
  4. Return true only if all conditions pass; otherwise return false.
Related Features: Game.hasSwapped, Game.board, Board.size, Tile.colour
Related Classes: Move, Board, Tile, Colour
Input:
  - move : Move — the candidate move to validate.
  - turn : int — the upcoming turn number (1-based).
  - board : Board — the current board state.
Output: boolean — true iff the move is legal to apply right now.
Pre-condition: board must be initialised; turn must be ≥ 1.
Post-condition: No state is mutated by the validation itself.

Game::endGame
Summary: Finalises the match with a given terminal status (WIN / TIMEOUT / BAD_MOVE / FAILED_LOAD) and returns a result map containing player names, winner, win method, per-player move times and turn counts, total turns and total game time.
Algorithm:
  1. Determine player1Name = player1.name, player2Name = player2.name.
  2. Determine winnerName: if status == WIN, return the name of the current player (whose colour equals board.winner). For TIMEOUT or BAD_MOVE, the winner is the opponent of the player who caused the end. For FAILED_LOAD, the winner string is empty.
  3. Compute winMethod: the string representation of the EndState.
  4. Read player1.moveTime, player2.moveTime, player1.turn, player2.turn.
  5. Compute totalTurns = player1.turn + player2.turn (or a single shared turn counter if maintained in Game.turn).
  6. Compute totalTime = nsToS(System.nanoTime() - startTime).
  7. Call formatResult(...) with all the values and return the resulting EMap.
Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.board, Game.startTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.formatResult, Game.nsToS, Player.name, Player.moveTime, Player.turn
Related Classes: Player, Board, Colour, EndState
Input: status : EndState — the reason the game ended.
Output: EMap — a map keyed by the strings player1, player2, winner, win_method, player1_move_time, player2_move_time, player1_turns, player2_turns, total_turns, total_game_time.
Pre-condition: The Game has been initialised via init(); players and a board exist; if status is WIN, board.winner must already be set.
Post-condition: The game is marked as ended; subsequent calls to play() should not continue the match.

Game::run
Summary: High-level entry point that prepares the game (loading the board, optionally from a string via HexHelper) and then invokes play() to produce the final result. Acts as the public façade for starting a match.
Algorithm:
  1. If a preloaded board string is provided (or set on Game), call helper.boardFromString(...) to build the board; otherwise create a fresh board via helper.createBoard(boardSize).
  2. Reset timing and turn counters.
  3. Delegate to play().
  4. Return the resulting EMap.
Related Features: Game.board, Game.helper, Game.play, Game.init
Related Classes: HexHelper, Board, Player
Input: None
Output: EMap — the same result map produced by play().
Pre-condition: init(...) must have been called successfully with two players and a board size.
Post-condition: The match has been executed; a final result map is returned.

# Requirement
```text
## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system models the board game Hex. Two players, RED and BLUE, take turns placing stones on a hexagonal grid represented as a square two-dimensional array. RED wins by forming a connected path from the top edge of the board to the bottom edge. BLUE wins by forming a connected path from the left edge to the right edge.

The implementation should focus on the core game model: board state, tiles, moves, players, agents, turn execution, win detection, and game result reporting. The board uses hex-style adjacency, so each tile has up to six neighbours rather than four. Empty cells are represented by Colour.EMPTY, separately from RED and BLUE stones.

The code should follow the class structure given in the UML. Constructors and ordinary accessors may be implemented as needed for object initialization and testing, but the main behavior should be driven by the model classes and their business responsibilities.

## 2. Functional Requirement

`Colour` represents cell/player colour states: EMPTY, RED, and BLUE. RED and BLUE are the two player sides; EMPTY represents an unoccupied board cell or no winner.

`Move` represents a position selected by an agent. A normal move stores board coordinates. The special coordinate pair `(-1, -1)` represents a swap move. Its string representation must be `"SWAP()"`; ordinary moves must be formatted as `"(x=<x>, y=<y>)"`.

`Tile` represents one board cell. It stores its coordinate, current colour, visited state, and the six neighbour displacement directions used by the Hex grid. A tile can be marked visited and later cleared. The six neighbour offsets should correspond to the standard slanted Hex board layout.

`Board` owns the board size, all tiles, and the winner if one has been found. It should support placing a colour at a coordinate, printing the board, cloning board state, clearing traversal marks, and detecting whether a colour has won. Win detection should search through connected same-colour tiles using the six Hex-neighbour directions. RED starts from the top edge and tries to reach the bottom edge; BLUE starts from the left edge and tries to reach the right edge. Board printing should use `"R"` for RED, `"B"` for BLUE, and `"0"` for empty cells, with cells separated by spaces and rows separated by newlines.

`AgentBase` is the base abstraction for game agents. Each agent has a colour and must provide a move when given the current turn, a board copy, and the opponent's previous move. It should also be able to determine the opposite colour. An invalid or missing colour should be treated as an error when asking for the opposite colour.

`Player` stores the participant name, the associated agent, accumulated move time, and number of turns taken.

`Game` coordinates a match between two players. It initializes the board, starts RED as the first player, alternates turns, asks the current agent for a move, validates moves, applies legal moves, handles the swap move on the second turn, checks for wins, tracks each player's elapsed move time, and ends the game when a player wins, times out, or makes an invalid move. The per-player time limit is five minutes expressed in nanoseconds. Time values reported to users should be converted from nanoseconds to seconds with millisecond precision.

The game result should be returned as a map of strings that includes the player names, winner, win method, per-player move times, per-player turn counts, total turns, and total game time. The keys expected by clients are `player1`, `player2`, `winner`, `win_method`, `player1_move_time`, `player2_move_time`, `player1_turns`, `player2_turns`, `total_turns`, and `total_game_time`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

`HexHelper` is an external helper class and should not be generated from the main UML. It provides colour conversion using `"R"`, `"B"`, and `"0"`, opposite-colour lookup, fully initialized board creation, and board loading from newline-separated, space-separated text.
```

# Operation Specifications
Game::play
Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
  2. Loop:
       a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
       b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
       c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.
       d. If active player's moveTime > maximumTime, call endGame(TIMEOUT) and break.
       e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.
       f. Call makeMove(move).
       g. Increment turn.
       h. If board.hasEnded(currentPlayer) is true after the opponent's move, call endGame(WIN) and break.
  3. Return the result map from endGame.
Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.startTime, Game.maximumTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.board, Game.turn, Board.clone, Board.hasEnded, AgentBase.makeMove, Player.moveTime, Player.agent
Related Classes: AgentBase, Player, Board, Move, Colour, EndState
Input: None
Output: EMap — the result of endGame containing the required player1, player2, winner, win_method, player1_move_time, player2_move_time, player1_turns, player2_turns, total_turns, total_game_time entries.
Pre-condition: init(...) must have been called; the players must have valid agents with valid colours.
Post-condition: A complete result map is returned and the game is in an ended state.

  { ... /* omitted */ }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Determines whether the given colour has won by forming a connected path between its designated board edges. It also returns true when the board has previously recorded the same winning colour.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If board.winner is non-EMPTY and is not the supplied colour, return false.
   *   2. If board.winner equals the supplied colour, return true.
   *   3. Invoke clearTiles() to remove traversal marks from any earlier search.
   *   4. If colour is RED, perform a six-neighbour DFS from every RED tile in the top row and determine whether the bottom row is reachable. If colour is BLUE, perform the corresponding search from every BLUE tile in the left column and determine whether the right column is reachable.
   *   5. If the target edge is reached, set winner to colour and return true; otherwise return false.
   * Related Features: Board.winner, Board.tiles, Board.size, Board.dfsColour, Board.clearTiles, Tile.visited, Tile.colour
   * Related Classes: Tile, Colour
   * Input:
   *   - colour : Colour — the player colour to test; normally RED or BLUE.
   * Output: boolean — true if that colour has connected its starting edge to its target edge, including when that colour is already stored as the winner; false if it has not or if a different colour is already the winner.

   * Summary: Determines whether the given colour has won by forming a connected path between its designated board edges. It also returns true when the board has previously recorded the same winning colour.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If board.winner is non-EMPTY and is not the supplied colour, return false.
   *   2. If board.winner equals the supplied colour, return true.
   *   3. Invoke clearTiles() to remove traversal marks from any earlier search.
   *   4. If colour is RED, perform a six-neighbour DFS from every RED tile in the top row and determine whether the bottom row is reachable. If colour is BLUE, perform the corresponding search from every BLUE tile in the left column and determine whether the right column is reachable.
   *   5. If the target edge is reached, set winner to colour and return true; otherwise return false.
   * Related Features: Board.winner, Board.tiles, Board.size, Board.dfsColour, Board.clearTiles, Tile.visited, Tile.colour
   * Related Classes: Tile, Colour
   * Input:
   *   - colour : Colour — the player colour to test; normally RED or BLUE.
   * Output: boolean — true if that colour has connected its starting edge to its target edge, including when that colour is already stored as the winner; false if it has not or if a different colour is already the winner.
   * Pre-condition: The board must be fully initialised, and colour must be RED or BLUE.

   * Summary: Determines whether the given colour has won by forming a connected path between its designated board edges. It also returns true when the board has previously recorded the same winning colour.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If board.winner is non-EMPTY and is not the supplied colour, return false.
   *   2. If board.winner equals the supplied colour, return true.
   *   3. Invoke clearTiles() to remove traversal marks from any earlier search.
   *   4. If colour is RED, perform a six-neighbour DFS from every RED tile in the top row and determine whether the bottom row is reachable. If colour is BLUE, perform the corresponding search from every BLUE tile in the left column and determine whether the right column is reachable.
   *   5. If the target edge is reached, set winner to colour and return true; otherwise return false.
   * Related Features: Board.winner, Board.tiles, Board.size, Board.dfsColour, Board.clearTiles, Tile.visited, Tile.colour
   * Related Classes: Tile, Colour
   * Input:

   * Output: boolean — true if that colour has connected its starting edge to its target edge, including when that colour is already stored as the winner; false if it has not or if a different colour is already the winner.

   *   1. If board.winner is non-EMPTY and is not the supplied colour, return false.

   * Summary: Determines whether the given colour has won by forming a connected path between its designated board edges. It also returns true when the board has previously recorded the same winning colour.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If board.winner is non-EMPTY and is not the supplied colour, return false.
   *   2. If board.winner equals the supplied colour, return true.
   *   3. Invoke clearTiles() to remove traversal marks from any earlier search.
   *   4. If colour is RED, perform a six-neighbour DFS from every RED tile in the top row and determine whether the bottom row is reachable. If colour is BLUE, perform the corresponding search from every BLUE tile in the left column and determine whether the right column is reachable.

   * Related Features: Board.winner, Board.tiles, Board.size, Board.dfsColour, Board.clearTiles, Tile.visited, Tile.colour
   * Related Classes: Tile, Colour
   * Input:

   * Output: boolean — true if that colour has connected its starting edge to its target edge, including when that colour is already stored as the winner; false if it has not or if a different colour is already the winner.
   * Pre-condition: The board must be fully initialised, and colour must be RED or BLUE.
   * Post-condition: The traversal marks generated by this check remain on the board. If a win is found, Board.winner is set to colour; otherwise, the existing winner state is preserved.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Performs a depth-first search over the board, starting from coordinate (x, y), visiting only tiles whose colour matches the supplied colour. This is the core traversal used by win detection.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If (x, y) is out of bounds, return.
   *   2. Retrieve the Tile at (x, y). If the tile is null, already visited, or has a different colour, return.
   *   3. Mark the tile as visited (visit()).
   *   4. For each of the six neighbour offsets (iDisp[k], jDisp[k]) of the current tile, recursively call dfsColour(x + iDisp[k], y + jDisp[k], colour).
   * Related Features: Board.tiles, Tile.iDisplacements, Tile.jDisplacements, Tile.visited, Tile.visit, Tile.colour
   * Related Classes: Tile
   * Input:
   *   - x : int — column coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
   *   - y : int — row coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Determines whether the given colour has won by forming a connected path between its designated board edges. It also returns true when the board has previously recorded the same winning colour.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If board.winner is non-EMPTY and is not the supplied colour, return false.
   *   2. If board.winner equals the supplied colour, return true.
   *   3. Invoke clearTiles() to remove traversal marks from any earlier search.
   *   4. If colour is RED, perform a six-neighbour DFS from every RED tile in the top row and determine whether the bottom row is reachable. If colour is BLUE, perform the corresponding search from every BLUE tile in the left column and determine whether the right column is reachable.
   *   5. If the target edge is reached, set winner to colour and return true; otherwise return false.
   * Related Features: Board.winner, Board.tiles, Board.size, Board.dfsColour, Board.clearTiles, Tile.visited, Tile.colour
   * Related Classes: Tile, Colour
   * Input:
   *   - colour : Colour — the player colour to test; normally RED or BLUE.
   * Output: boolean — true if that colour has connected its starting edge to its target edge, including when that colour is already stored as the winner; false if it has not or if a different colour is already the winner.
   * Pre-condition: The board must be fully initialised, and colour must be RED or BLUE.
   * Post-condition: The traversal marks generated by this check remain on the board. If a win is found, Board.winner is set to colour; otherwise, the existing winner state is preserved.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model required="true" colourRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean hasEnded(final Colour colour)
  {
    if (winner != Colour.EMPTY && winner != colour)
      return false;
    if (winner == colour)
      return true;

    clearTiles();

    int n = size;
    boolean targetReached = false;

    if (colour == Colour.RED)
    {
      for (int x = 0; x < n; x++)
      {
        Tile t = tiles[x][0];
        if (t != null && t.getColour() == Colour.RED)
          dfsColour(x, 0, colour);
      }
      for (int x = 0; x < n; x++)
      {
        Tile t = tiles[x][n - 1];
        if (t != null && t.isVisited())
        {
          targetReached = true;
          break;
        }
      }
    }
    else if (colour == Colour.BLUE)
    {
      for (int y = 0; y < n; y++)
      {
        Tile t = tiles[0][y];
        if (t != null && t.getColour() == Colour.BLUE)
          dfsColour(0, y, colour);
      }
      for (int y = 0; y < n; y++)
      {
        Tile t = tiles[n - 1][y];
        if (t != null && t.isVisited())
        {
          targetReached = true;
          break;
        }
      }
    }

    if (targetReached)
      setWinner(colour);

    return targetReached;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Performs a depth-first search over the board, starting from coordinate (x, y), visiting only tiles whose colour matches the supplied colour. This is the core traversal used by win detection.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If (x, y) is out of bounds, return.
   *   2. Retrieve the Tile at (x, y). If the tile is null, already visited, or has a different colour, return.
   *   3. Mark the tile as visited (visit()).
   *   4. For each of the six neighbour offsets (iDisp[k], jDisp[k]) of the current tile, recursively call dfsColour(x + iDisp[k], y + jDisp[k], colour).
   * Related Features: Board.tiles, Tile.iDisplacements, Tile.jDisplacements, Tile.visited, Tile.visit, Tile.colour
   * Related Classes: Tile
   * Input:
   *   - x : int — column coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
   *   - y : int — row coordinate (0-based) of the starting tile.
   *   - colour : Colour — the colour whose connectivity is being explored (RED or BLUE).
   * Output: void
   * Pre-condition: Board tiles must be initialised; (x, y) should be in-range and contain the given colour for a meaningful call. Traversal marks should typically be cleared beforehand via clearTiles().
   * Post-condition: All tiles reachable from (x, y) via same-colour six-way adjacency are marked visited; the board's logical state is unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model xRequired="true" yRequired="true" colourRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void dfsColour(final int x, final int y, final Colour colour)
  {
    if (x < 0 || x >= size || y < 0 || y >= size)
      return;
    Tile t = tiles[x][y];
    if (t == null || t.isVisited() || t.getColour() != colour)
      return;
    t.visit();
    int[] iDisp = t.getIDisplacements();
    int[] jDisp = t.getJDisplacements();
    int count = t.getNEIGHBOUR_COUNT();
    for (int k = 0; k < count; k++)
      dfsColour(x + iDisp[k], y + jDisp[k], colour);
  }

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
   *   2. Loop:
   *        a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
   *        b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
   *        c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.
   *        d. If active player's moveTime > maximumTime, call endGame(TIMEOUT) and break.
   *        e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.
   *        f. Call makeMove(move).
   *        g. Increment turn.
   *        h. If board.hasEnded(currentPlayer) is true after the opponent's move, call endGame(WIN) and break.
   *   3. Return the result map from endGame.
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.startTime, Game.maximumTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.board, Game.turn, Board.clone, Board.hasEnded, AgentBase.makeMove, Player.moveTime, Player.agent

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
   *   2. Loop:
   *        a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
   *        b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
   *        c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.
   *        d. If active player's moveTime > maximumTime, call endGame(TIMEOUT) and break.
   *        e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.
   *        f. Call makeMove(move).
   *        g. Increment turn.
   *        h. If board.hasEnded(currentPlayer) is true after the opponent's move, call endGame(WIN) and break.
   *   3. Return the result map from endGame.
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.startTime, Game.maximumTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.board, Game.turn, Board.clone, Board.hasEnded, AgentBase.makeMove, Player.moveTime, Player.agent
   * Related Classes: AgentBase, Player, Board, Move, Colour, EndState
   * Input: None
   * Output: EMap — the result of endGame containing the required player1, player2, winner, win_method, player1_move_time, player2_move_time, player1_turns, player2_turns, total_turns, total_game_time entries.
   * Pre-condition: init(...) must have been called; the players must have valid agents with valid colours.
   * Post-condition: A complete result map is returned and the game is in an ended state.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Applies a legal move to the game state, updating the board, advancing the turn, toggling the current player, and recording that the swap move has been used if applicable.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If move represents the swap (x == -1 and y == -1):
   *        a. Locate the opponent's last move (the only RED tile on the board, since only RED has moved on turn 1).
   *        b. Set the current player's colour on that tile (i.e., the second player takes over RED's first stone).
   *        c. Set hasSwapped = true.
   *   2. Otherwise (normal move):
   *        a. Call board.setTileColour(move.x, move.y, currentPlayer).
   *   3. Increment the current player's turn counter (player1.turn or player2.turn) and Game.turn.
   *   4. Flip currentPlayer to its opposite (RED ↔ BLUE).
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.board, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.turn, Board.setTileColour, Player.turn
   * Related Classes: Move, Board, Tile, Colour, Player

   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Determine player1Name = player1.name, player2Name = player2.name.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Checks whether a move is legal in the current game context, considering the turn number, board state and the special swap rule.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If move is null, return false.
   *   2. If turn == 2 and move represents the swap move (x == -1 and y == -1) and hasSwapped is false, return true (the second player may swap with the first player's first move).
   *   3. Otherwise, the move must be a normal coordinate move:
   *        a. x and y must be within 0..board.size-1.
   *        b. The tile at (x, y) must currently be EMPTY.
   *   4. Return true only if all conditions pass; otherwise return false.
   * Related Features: Game.hasSwapped, Game.board, Board.size, Tile.colour

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
   *   2. Loop:
   *        a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
   *        b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
   *        c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.
   *        d. If active player's moveTime > maximumTime, call endGame(TIMEOUT) and break.
   *        e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.
   *        f. Call makeMove(move).
   *        g. Increment turn.
   *        h. If board.hasEnded(currentPlayer) is true after the opponent's move, call endGame(WIN) and break.
   *   3. Return the result map from endGame.
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.startTime, Game.maximumTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.board, Game.turn, Board.clone, Board.hasEnded, AgentBase.makeMove, Player.moveTime, Player.agent
   * Related Classes: AgentBase, Player, Board, Move, Colour, EndState
   * Input: None
   * Output: EMap — the result of endGame containing the required player1, player2, winner, win_method, player1_move_time, player2_move_time, player1_turns, player2_turns, total_turns, total_game_time entries.
   * Pre-condition: init(...) must have been called; the players must have valid agents with valid colours.
   * Post-condition: A complete result map is returned and the game is in an ended state.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Applies a legal move to the game state, updating the board, advancing the turn, toggling the current player, and recording that the swap move has been used if applicable.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If move represents the swap (x == -1 and y == -1):
   *        a. Locate the opponent's last move (the only RED tile on the board, since only RED has moved on turn 1).
   *        b. Set the current player's colour on that tile (i.e., the second player takes over RED's first stone).
   *        c. Set hasSwapped = true.
   *   2. Otherwise (normal move):
   *        a. Call board.setTileColour(move.x, move.y, currentPlayer).
   *   3. Increment the current player's turn counter (player1.turn or player2.turn) and Game.turn.
   *   4. Flip currentPlayer to its opposite (RED ↔ BLUE).
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.board, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.turn, Board.setTileColour, Player.turn
   * Related Classes: Move, Board, Tile, Colour, Player

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Finalises the match with a given terminal status (WIN / TIMEOUT / BAD_MOVE / FAILED_LOAD) and returns a result map containing player names, winner, win method, per-player move times and turn counts, total turns and total game time.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Determine player1Name = player1.name, player2Name = player2.name.
   *   2. Determine winnerName: if status == WIN, return the name of the current player (whose colour equals board.winner). For TIMEOUT or BAD_MOVE, the winner is the opponent of the player who caused the end. For FAILED_LOAD, the winner string is empty.
   *   3. Compute winMethod: the string representation of the EndState.
   *   4. Read player1.moveTime, player2.moveTime, player1.turn, player2.turn.
   *   5. Compute totalTurns = player1.turn + player2.turn (or a single shared turn counter if maintained in Game.turn).
   *   6. Compute totalTime = nsToS(System.nanoTime() - startTime).
   *   7. Call formatResult(...) with all the values and return the resulting EMap.
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.board, Game.startTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.formatResult, Game.nsToS, Player.name, Player.moveTime, Player.turn

   * Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
   *   2. Loop:
   *        a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
   *        b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
   *        c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.
   *        d. If active player's moveTime > maximumTime, call endGame(TIMEOUT) and break.
   *        e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.
   *        f. Call makeMove(move).
   *        g. Increment turn.
   *        h. If board.hasEnded(currentPlayer) is true after the opponent's move, call endGame(WIN) and break.

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
   *   2. Loop:
   *        a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
   *        b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
   *        c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.
   *        d. If active player's moveTime > maximumTime, call endGame(TIMEOUT) and break.
   *        e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.
   *        f. Call makeMove(move).
   *        g. Increment turn.
   *        h. If board.hasEnded(currentPlayer) is true after the opponent's move, call endGame(WIN) and break.
   *   3. Return the result map from endGame.

   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If move is null, return false.
   *   2. If turn == 2 and move represents the swap move (x == -1 and y == -1) and hasSwapped is false, return true (the second player may swap with the first player's first move).

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
   *   2. Loop:
   *        a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
   *        b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
   *        c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.
   *        d. If active player's moveTime > maximumTime, call endGame(TIMEOUT) and break.
   *        e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.
   *        f. Call makeMove(move).
   *        g. Increment turn.
   *        h. If board.hasEnded(currentPlayer) is true after the opponent's move, call endGame(WIN) and break.
   *   3. Return the result map from endGame.
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.startTime, Game.maximumTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.board, Game.turn, Board.clone, Board.hasEnded, AgentBase.makeMove, Player.moveTime, Player.agent
   * Related Classes: AgentBase, Player, Board, Move, Colour, EndState

   * Pre-condition: init(...) must have been called; the players must have valid agents with valid colours.

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Applies a legal move to the game state, updating the board, advancing the turn, toggling the current player, and recording that the swap move has been used if applicable.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If move represents the swap (x == -1 and y == -1):
   *        a. Locate the opponent's last move (the only RED tile on the board, since only RED has moved on turn 1).

   * Algorithm:

   * Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
   *   2. Loop:
   *        a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
   *        b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
   *        c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.

   *        e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Checks whether a move is legal in the current game context, considering the turn number, board state and the special swap rule.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If move is null, return false.
   *   2. If turn == 2 and move represents the swap move (x == -1 and y == -1) and hasSwapped is false, return true (the second player may swap with the first player's first move).
   *   3. Otherwise, the move must be a normal coordinate move:
   *        a. x and y must be within 0..board.size-1.
   *        b. The tile at (x, y) must currently be EMPTY.
   *   4. Return true only if all conditions pass; otherwise return false.
   * Related Features: Game.hasSwapped, Game.board, Board.size, Tile.colour
   * Related Classes: Move, Board, Tile, Colour
   * Input:
   *   - move : Move — the candidate move to validate.
   *   - turn : int — the upcoming turn number (1-based).
   *   - board : Board — the current board state.
   * Output: boolean — true iff the move is legal to apply right now.
   * Pre-condition: board must be initialised; turn must be ≥ 1.
   * Post-condition: No state is mutated by the validation itself.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model required="true" turnRequired="true" boardRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean isValidMove(final Move move, final int turn, final Board board)
  {
    if (move == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (turn == 2 && move.getX() == -1 && move.getY() == -1 && !isHasSwapped())
    {
      return true;
    }
    int size = board.getSize();
    int x = move.getX();
    int y = move.getY();
    if (x < 0 || x >= size || y < 0 || y >= size)
    {
      return false;
    }
    Tile[][] tiles = board.getTiles();
    if (tiles == null || tiles[x][y] == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (tiles[x][y].getColour() != Colour.EMPTY)
    {
      return false;
    }
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Drives the main game loop: alternating turns, asking the active agent for a move, validating and applying it, timing it, and ending the game when a win, timeout, or invalid move occurs. Returns the final result map.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialise currentPlayer = RED, turn = 1, hasSwapped = false, startTime = System.nanoTime().
   *   2. Loop:
   *        a. Identify the active Player (player1 if currentPlayer == RED, else player2) and their AgentBase.
   *        b. Record t0 = System.nanoTime(); compute tElapsed = (t0 - startTime) / 2 (i.e. remaining time for the current player out of the 5-minute budget). Ask agent.makeMove(turn, board.clone(), opponent's last move or null).
   *        c. Record t1 = System.nanoTime(); add (t1 - t0) to active player's moveTime.
   *        d. If active player's moveTime > maximumTime, call endGame(TIMEOUT) and break.
   *        e. If !isValidMove(move, turn, board), call endGame(BAD_MOVE) and break.
   *        f. Call makeMove(move).
   *        g. Increment turn.
   *        h. If board.hasEnded(currentPlayer) is true after the opponent's move, call endGame(WIN) and break.
   *   3. Return the result map from endGame.
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.startTime, Game.maximumTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.board, Game.turn, Board.clone, Board.hasEnded, AgentBase.makeMove, Player.moveTime, Player.agent
   * Related Classes: AgentBase, Player, Board, Move, Colour, EndState
   * Input: None
   * Output: EMap — the result of endGame containing the required player1, player2, winner, win_method, player1_move_time, player2_move_time, player1_turns, player2_turns, total_turns, total_game_time entries.
   * Pre-condition: init(...) must have been called; the players must have valid agents with valid colours.
   * Post-condition: A complete result map is returned and the game is in an ended state.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<String, String> play()
  {
    setCurrentPlayer(Colour.RED);
    setTurn(1);
    setHasSwapped(false);
    setStartTime(System.nanoTime());

    Move opponentLastMove = null;
    Move lastMove = null;

    while (true)
    {
      Player activePlayer = (getCurrentPlayer() == Colour.RED) ? getPlayer1() : getPlayer2();
      Player opponent = (activePlayer == getPlayer1()) ? getPlayer2() : getPlayer1();
      AgentBase agent = activePlayer.getAgent();

      long t0 = System.nanoTime();
      Move move = agent.makeMove(getTurn(), getBoard().clone(), opponentLastMove);
      long t1 = System.nanoTime();

      activePlayer.setMoveTime(activePlayer.getMoveTime() + (t1 - t0));

      if (activePlayer.getMoveTime() > getMaximumTime())
      {
        return endGame(EndState.TIMEOUT);
      }

      if (!isValidMove(move, getTurn(), getBoard()))
      {
        return endGame(EndState.BAD_MOVE);
      }

      makeMove(move);
      lastMove = move;
      opponentLastMove = lastMove;
      setTurn(getTurn() + 1);

      if (getBoard().hasEnded(getCurrentPlayer()))
      {
        return endGame(EndState.WIN);
      }
    }
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Applies a legal move to the game state, updating the board, advancing the turn, toggling the current player, and recording that the swap move has been used if applicable.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If move represents the swap (x == -1 and y == -1):
   *        a. Locate the opponent's last move (the only RED tile on the board, since only RED has moved on turn 1).
   *        b. Set the current player's colour on that tile (i.e., the second player takes over RED's first stone).
   *        c. Set hasSwapped = true.
   *   2. Otherwise (normal move):
   *        a. Call board.setTileColour(move.x, move.y, currentPlayer).
   *   3. Increment the current player's turn counter (player1.turn or player2.turn) and Game.turn.
   *   4. Flip currentPlayer to its opposite (RED ↔ BLUE).
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.hasSwapped, Game.board, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.turn, Board.setTileColour, Player.turn
   * Related Classes: Move, Board, Tile, Colour, Player
   * Input: m : Move — the validated move to apply.
   * Output: void
   * Pre-condition: isValidMove(m, ...) must return true; the game must not already have ended.
   * Post-condition: The board reflects the move, the active colour has been switched, and the appropriate player's turn count is incremented.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void makeMove(final Move m)
  {
    if (m.getX() == -1 && m.getY() == -1)
    {
      Tile[][] tiles = getBoard().getTiles();
      for (int i = 0; i < tiles.length; i++)
      {
        for (int j = 0; j < tiles[i].length; j++)
        {
          if (tiles[i][j].getColour() == Colour.RED)
          {
            getBoard().setTileColour(i, j, getCurrentPlayer());
            break;
          }
        }
      }
      setHasSwapped(true);
    }
    else
    {
      getBoard().setTileColour(m.getX(), m.getY(), getCurrentPlayer());
    }

    if (getCurrentPlayer() == Colour.RED)
    {
      getPlayer1().setTurn(getPlayer1().getTurn() + 1);
    }
    else
    {
      getPlayer2().setTurn(getPlayer2().getTurn() + 1);
    }
    setTurn(getTurn() + 1);

    setCurrentPlayer(getHelper().colourOpposite(getCurrentPlayer()));
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Finalises the match with a given terminal status (WIN / TIMEOUT / BAD_MOVE / FAILED_LOAD) and returns a result map containing player names, winner, win method, per-player move times and turn counts, total turns and total game time.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Determine player1Name = player1.name, player2Name = player2.name.
   *   2. Determine winnerName: if status == WIN, return the name of the current player (whose colour equals board.winner). For TIMEOUT or BAD_MOVE, the winner is the opponent of the player who caused the end. For FAILED_LOAD, the winner string is empty.
   *   3. Compute winMethod: the string representation of the EndState.
   *   4. Read player1.moveTime, player2.moveTime, player1.turn, player2.turn.
   *   5. Compute totalTurns = player1.turn + player2.turn (or a single shared turn counter if maintained in Game.turn).
   *   6. Compute totalTime = nsToS(System.nanoTime() - startTime).
   *   7. Call formatResult(...) with all the values and return the resulting EMap.
   * Related Features: Game.currentPlayer, Game.board, Game.startTime, Game.player1, Game.player2, Game.formatResult, Game.nsToS, Player.name, Player.moveTime, Player.turn
   * Related Classes: Player, Board, Colour, EndState
   * Input: status : EndState — the reason the game ended.
   * Output: EMap — a map keyed by the strings player1, player2, winner, win_method, player1_move_time, player2_move_time, player1_turns, player2_turns, total_turns, total_game_time.
   * Pre-condition: The Game has been initialised via init(); players and a board exist; if status is WIN, board.winner must already be set.
   * Post-condition: The game is marked as ended; subsequent calls to play() should not continue the match.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<String, String> endGame(final EndState status)
  {
    String player1Name = getPlayer1().getName();
    String player2Name = getPlayer2().getName();
    String winnerName = "";
    if (status == EndState.WIN)
    {
      if (getBoard().getWinner() == Colour.RED)
      {
        winnerName = player1Name;
      }
      else if (getBoard().getWinner() == Colour.BLUE)
      {
        winnerName = player2Name;
      }
    }
    else if (status == EndState.TIMEOUT || status == EndState.BAD_MOVE)
    {
      if (getCurrentPlayer() == Colour.RED)
      {
        winnerName = player2Name;
      }
      else
      {
        winnerName = player1Name;
      }
    }
    String winMethod = status.getName();
    double player1MoveTime = nsToS(getPlayer1().getMoveTime());
    double player2MoveTime = nsToS(getPlayer2().getMoveTime());
    int player1Turn = getPlayer1().getTurn();
    int player2Turn = getPlayer2().getTurn();
    int totalTurns = player1Turn + player2Turn;
    double totalTime = nsToS(System.nanoTime() - getStartTime());
    return formatResult(player1Name, player2Name, winnerName, winMethod, player1MoveTime, player2MoveTime, player1Turn, player2Turn, totalTurns, totalTime);
  }

  public Map<String, String> formatResult(final String player1Name, final String player2Name, final String winner, final String winMethod, final double player1MoveTime, final double player2MoveTime, final int player1Turn, final int player2Turn, final double totalTurns, final double totalTime)
